- Modules (16.2): `src/allen_diameter/analysis/cell_alignment.py` (the projection choice, the two-stage alignment, its null and verdict; arrays in, records out), `src/allen_diameter/loading/survey_io.py` (the survey folder: per-cell context, step markers, the status table), `src/allen_diameter/plotting/survey_figures.py` (the overlay), `src/allen_diameter/config.py` (`SurveyConfig`'s `align_*` fields, in no signature), `scripts/survey_cells.py` (`run`, `status`; the step `align`), notebook Cell 12 and the runbook
- 16.2 notation: $\rho_0$ = `acquisition.res0_um` (0.1144 um/px), the full-resolution pixel; a downsample level $q$ has the factor $f_q = 2^q$ and the pixel $\rho_q = f_q\rho_0$; an SWC node $i$ at $(x_i, y_i)$ (um) maps to the full-resolution pixel $X_i = x_i/\rho_0 + \Delta x$, $Y_i = H - y_i/\rho_0 + \Delta y$ with a y-flip or $Y_i = y_i/\rho_0 + \Delta y$ without, $(\Delta x, \Delta y)$ the cell's shift (full-res px), $H$ the flip height (full-res px) -- Block 0's transform (`allen_image_align`, equations (1)-(2)), the one `cell.to_image_um` applies with `shift_full_px`, `flip_y_full_h`, and `z0_um` for the depth (plane index $= (z_i - z_0)/\Delta z$)
- 16.2 (a), the projection (D-085.2; the rule the assistant's, confirmed by the user, D-089; what the projections are: 16.3): the candidates are the cell's SubImages (`list_images`) other than Primary whose width and height each lie within `projection_size_rel_tol` (0.02) of the median width and height of the usable Primary planes (`plane_table`): the xy projections (Allen's yz projections are as wide as the stack is deep). Each is fetched whole at $q_c$ = `align_downsample` (4) [D-085.2]; the minimum-intensity projection is the candidate with the lowest median grey level (a maximum-intensity projection of a dark fill on a bright field is the brighter one) **[reasoning; the handoff names Allen's min-xy projection, `docs/handoff_diameter_remeasurement.md`, its `image_type` string not recorded]**; no candidate: the step fails, naming the SubImage types the cell has
- 16.2 (b), coarse (D-085.2, the user's method): `allen_image_align.align_translation(swc, P, rho_c, max_shift_um=align_max_shift_um, try_flip=True, refine_px=2)` on the projection $P$ at $q_c$ -- the FFT cross-correlation of the darkness map $D_c$ (`darkness_map`) with the skeleton mask of SWC types 3 and 4, both flips, then the $\pm$2 px refinement; returns the array shift $(d_x, d_y)$, the flip and the score $s_c$ = the mean of $D_c$ under the mask. Full resolution: $\Delta x = f_c d_x$, $\Delta y = f_c d_y$, $H = f_c\,n_{\rm rows}(P)$ when flipped (the flip axis the fit used; `allen_image_align.to_full_res_shift`). `at_border`: $\max(|d_x|, |d_y|) \ge M - 1$ with $M = \min(\lfloor$`align_max_shift_um`$/\rho_c\rfloor, n_{\rm rows}/2 - 1, n_{\rm cols}/2 - 1)$, the search's half-window
- 16.2 (c), the null (the assistant's, PROVISIONAL) [corrected 2026-10-26 with the smoke test: the draft's null translated the aligned mask rigidly, single placements against a score that is the maximum over the search window, and called an empty field of dark specks a cell ($R$ = 15)]: the observed $s^{\rm obs}$ = the best mean of $D_c$ under the mask over the coarse search -- `align_translation`'s FFT cross-correlation and window $|d_x|, |d_y| \le M$, both flips, without its refinement, divided by the mask's pixel count (`search_score`); the null = the same search on $n_0$ = `align_null_n` (50) copies of $D_c$ whose square tiles of side $b$ = round(`align_null_block_um`/$\rho_c$) px (30 um: 16 px at level 4) are permuted by `numpy.random.default_rng([selection_seed, specimen_id])` (the margins that fill no tile stay in place; `block_shuffle`, `shuffle_null`) -- the maximum the search reaches on the image's own content without the cell's geometry. $R = s^{\rm obs} / \mathrm{median}(\text{null})$, with the null's median and $1.4826\times$MAD reported; fewer than 10 finite null scores: $R$ = NaN
- 16.2 (d), fine (the assistant's addition, PROVISIONAL; reported beside the coarse result): the skeleton under the coarse transform, its full-res bounding box widened by `align_fine_margin_um` (20 um) and clipped to $P$'s full-res extent, fetched at $q_f$ = the smallest level $\ge$ `align_fine_downsample` (1) whose returned image passes Block 0's size guard (`check_request_size`; at most $q_c - 1$); $D_f$ = `allen_image_align.ridge_darkness(crop, rho_f, align_fine_ridge_um)` (6 um: dark features narrower than that, the soma removed); the mask $\mathcal M$ of the skeleton under the coarse transform in the crop's pixels (`render_skeleton_mask`, types 3 and 4); the score $S(a, b)$ = mean of $D_f$ over $\{(r + b, c + a) : (r, c) \in \mathcal M\}$ inside the crop, for every integer $(a, b)$ with $|a|, |b| \le A$, $A = \lfloor$`align_fine_max_shift_um`$/\rho_f\rfloor$ ($\pm$5 um) -- a direct search (Custom: `align_translation`'s FFT pads the crop to twice its size, gigabytes at $q_f = 1$, and its window is global); $(a^*, b^*) = \arg\max S$; $\Delta x \mathrel{+}= f_f a^*$, $\Delta y \mathrel{+}= f_f b^*$. `fine_at_border`: $\max(|a^*|, |b^*|) = A$. Block 0's frame puts array pixel $c$ at full-res $f c$, the corner of the block a box-averaging server reduces into it, so a stage at level $q$ places the skeleton up to $(f_q - 1)/2$ px short of the content: about 8 px at level 4, which the fine stage removes, and 0.5 px (0.06 um) at level 1, which stays **[run, sandbox: the synthetic cells' errors, all negative]**
- 16.2 (e), the verdict and the record: $z_0$ = `align_z0_um` (0.0 um, as 529878215: plane index $= z/\Delta z$; PROVISIONAL, to be checked by S3b's registration offsets $\Delta z^*$). The cell is set aside (D-085.2) when $R <$ `align_min_score_ratio` (2.0, the assistant's, confirmed by the user, D-089) or $R$ is NaN, or `at_border`, or `fine_at_border`; the reasons are listed. Record (`alignment_<sid>.json`): `specimen_id`, `method` (`auto` or `manual`), `shift_x_px`, `shift_y_px`, `flip_h_px` (None without a flip), `z0_um`, `coarse` {`downsample`, `dx_px`, `dy_px` (array px), `flip`, `score`, `score_noflip`, `score_flip`, `half_window_px`, `at_border`}, `null` {`n`, `block_px`, `observed`, `median`, `mad`}, `score_ratio`, `fine` {`downsample`, `crop` (left, top, width, height, full-res px), `dx_px`, `dy_px` (crop px), `score`, `score_coarse` ($S(0, 0)$), `half_window_px`, `at_border`}, `projection` {`id`, `image_type`, `candidates` [{`id`, `image_type`, `width`, `height`, `median_gl`}]}, `set_aside`, `reasons`. A manual alignment, `<cell>/align/alignment_manual.json` with `shift_x_px`, `shift_y_px`, `flip_h_px`, `z0_um`, is used as given (`method` `manual`, not set aside); the user writes it after looking at a set-aside cell's overlay
- 16.2 (f), the overlay (`plotting/survey_figures.alignment_overlay`; D-085.2): two panels -- the projection at $q_c$ with the skeleton under the final transform, and the fine crop at $q_f$ with the skeleton under the coarse and the final transforms -- titled with the specimen, the shifts, the flip, $R$ and the verdict; `align/overlay_<sid>.png`
- 16.2 (g), the runner (`scripts/survey_cells.py run`; D-085.12): the cells of the selection (Block 16.1, `selection.csv`, in its order) and the named steps, in the order the step list gives them. Folder: `<out>/<sid>/` per cell, holding `cell.json` (the selection record, the SWC path, the alignment once known) and `done/<step>.json`, the marker of a finished step (its summary, the time in s, the configuration's full hash, `finished_utc`), written atomically after the step's own files; `<out>/status.csv`, one row per cell: `specimen_id`, `group`, then per step `done`, `failed`, `skipped:<why>` or `pending`, and `error` (the last failure's message). For each cell and step: a step with a marker is skipped (`done`) unless `--force` names it; when the cell's alignment set it aside, every step after `align` is `skipped:set_aside` unless `--include-set-aside`; otherwise the step runs, and an exception is caught, recorded as `failed` with its type and message, the cell's later steps are `skipped:after_failure`, and the runner goes on to the next cell. A failed step leaves no marker, so the next run retries it. `status` prints and rewrites the table from the markers alone. The steps are named functions `step(ctx, cfg) -> dict` registered in the script (16.2: `align`; Blocks 17-19 add theirs); `ctx` carries the cell's record, folder, the Allen source and the alignment
- Allen source (16.2): the step functions reach Allen only through a source object with `swc_path(sid)`, `images(sid)` (the `list_images` table), `planes(sid)` (`plane_table` of it; added with Block 17) and `fetcher` (an `ImageFetcher`); the real one wraps `allen_image_io` (`fetch_swc`, `list_images`, `HttpFetcher` with the cache); the smoke test substitutes a synthetic one
- Public API (16.2): `cell_alignment.xy_projections(images, rel_tol) -> DataFrame`; `cell_alignment.darkest(images) -> int`; `cell_alignment.coarse_alignment(swc, P, rho_c, f_c, scfg) -> dict`; `cell_alignment.search_masks(swc, shape, rho) -> [mask, mask_flipped]`; `cell_alignment.search_score(D, masks, M) -> float`; `cell_alignment.block_shuffle(D, block_px, rng) -> ndarray`; `cell_alignment.shuffle_null(D, masks, M, block_px, rng, n) -> ndarray`; `cell_alignment.fine_search(D, mask, half_px) -> (a, b, S, S00)`; `cell_alignment.fine_crop(swc, transform, rho0, margin_um, extent) -> (left, top, width, height)`; `cell_alignment.verdict(record, scfg) -> (set_aside, reasons)`; `cell_alignment.full_transform(record) -> dict` (`shift_full_px`, `flip_y_full_h`, `z0_um`: the keyword arguments of `cell.to_image_um`); `survey_io.CellDir`, `survey_io.write_marker`, `survey_io.read_marker`, `survey_io.write_status`, `survey_io.read_selection`; `survey_figures.alignment_overlay(...) -> Figure`; `scripts/survey_cells.py run --selection SEL.csv --out-dir OUT --cache-dir C [--steps align] [--cells IDS] [--force STEPS] [--include-set-aside] [--config-json]`, `status --out-dir OUT`
- Parameters (`SurveyConfig`, in no signature): `align_downsample` 4 [D-085.2], `align_max_shift_um` 250 (Block 0's default), `align_null_n` 50, `align_null_block_um` 30, `align_min_score_ratio` 2.0, `align_fine_downsample` 1, `align_fine_margin_um` 20, `align_fine_max_shift_um` 5, `align_fine_ridge_um` 6, `align_z0_um` 0.0, `projection_size_rel_tol` 0.02 -- all PROVISIONAL but the first and `align_min_score_ratio` (confirmed by the user, D-089)
- Library calls relied on: `allen_image_align.align_translation`, `darkness_map`, `ridge_darkness`, `render_skeleton_mask`, `swc_to_full_px`; `allen_image_io.fetch_whole`, `fetch_crop`, `check_request_size`, `list_images`, `plane_table`, `fetch_swc`; `allen_image_plot.read_swc`, `overlay_swc`, `show_image`; numpy (`random.default_rng`, `median`); scipy through Block 0 (`signal.fftconvolve`, `ndimage`)
- Custom code: the fine stage's direct search (above); the block-shuffle null (no library offers a max-statistic null for a skeleton search)
- Test oracles (`tests/smoke/test_smoke_cell_alignment.py`): (a) known answer: a synthetic cell -- a tree of dendrites painted as dark tubes on a bright noisy field at a known full-res shift, with its maximum-intensity twin and a yz-shaped image among the SubImages, served by a synthetic source -- gives back the minimum projection, the flip state, $\Delta x$ and $\Delta y$ within $f_f + 0.5$ full-res px after the fine stage (its pixel and the half-pixel corner offset) and within $1.5 f_c$ after the coarse one, $R \ge$ 2, not set aside; the painted y-flipped cell gives the flip and $H = f_c n_{\rm rows}$; (b) reference: the final transform maps the skeleton onto the painted truth -- `allen_image_align.swc_to_full_px` with the result against the transform that painted it, every dendrite node within $(f_f + 0.5)\sqrt 2$ px; (c) invariants: painting the cell $k$ = 37 full-res px further along x (the same noise and specks) moves $\Delta x$ by $k$ within $f_f$; (d) contract: the record's keys and JSON round trip; the marker and the status table; `full_transform` feeds `cell.to_image_um`; (e) determinism: the null with its seed gives the same $R$ twice; (f) edge cases: an empty field (no cell painted) is set aside on $R$; a manual alignment is used as given; a cell without an xy projection fails the step with the types named; the runner: a done step is skipped, `--force` reruns it, a failing step is recorded and the next cell runs, a set-aside cell skips its later steps and `--include-set-aside` runs them, `status` rebuilds the table from the markers
- Data flow (16.2): consumes 16.1's `selection.csv` and Block 0; feeds every later S3 step the cell's transform (`full_transform`) and the set-aside verdict
- Status (16.2): drafted 2026-10-26; implemented 2026-10-26 (`analysis/cell_alignment.py`, `loading/survey_io.py`, `plotting/survey_figures.py`, `SurveyConfig.align_*`, `scripts/survey_cells.py` `run`/`status` with the step `align`, notebook Cell 12 and runbook v25; the estimator and simulation hashes unchanged). `test_smoke_cell_alignment.py` 6 pass, 1 skip (`test_convergence`: no discretisation), 30 s: on painted cells the final shift within 2.5 full-res px (errors -1 to -2 px, all negative: the corner offset), the coarse within 13 px, R 6.9-7.1; an empty field R 1.08, set aside; 20 of 20 mutants each failed a check (the projection's size test and darkest rule, the full-res scaling of the shift, the flip height and the fine step, the window edge, the shuffle, the ratio, the fine search's axes, the crop frame, the verdict, the fine level's size guard, the runner's resume, failure, set-aside, force and context rules, the manual alignment, the null's seed, `validate`) **[run, sandbox]**. The null of the draft (rigid translations) was replaced by the block-shuffle null when the smoke test's empty field scored R = 15 (see (c)). Not run against Allen's server (Colab Cell 12): the projections' `image_type`, R on real cells and the threshold are unknown until then. Smoke-tested
- Modules (16.3, added 2026-10-27; D-090.1): `src/allen_diameter/analysis/projection_check.py` (the operators over the planes, the offset search, the verdict, the window; arrays in, records out), `src/allen_image_io.py` (`subimage_rows` and `images_frame`: `list_images` split in two, its table unchanged), `src/allen_diameter/plotting/survey_figures.py` (`projection_check_figure`), `src/allen_diameter/config.py` (`SurveyConfig.projcheck_*`, in no signature), `scripts/projection_check.py`, notebook Cell 11b and the runbook
- 16.3, what Allen's projections are (D-090.1: the user, 2026-10-27, "understand this by yourself. If not possible write a colab function and cell to run on colab"): the sandbox cannot reach Allen's server, and a WebFetch of the RMA query waits for the user's approval (withdrawn unanswered, 2026-10-27), so the check runs in Colab. Known before it **[KB-repo: `docs/handoff_diameter_remeasurement.md`, from live API queries of the design chat, 2026-09-23 to 09-30, not re-queried]**: specimen 529878215 has four projections, named there min-xy (546843114, 7607 x 9435 px), min-yz (546843119, 1333 x 9435), max-xy (550303812) and max-yz (550303817), and Primary planes of 7605 x 9433 px; the `image_type` strings were not recorded. Under 16.2 (a)'s rules the xy pair are the candidates and the darkest is the min-xy, if the names say what the images are -- which (b) tests on the pixels
- 16.3 (a), the listing (per specimen of `--specimens` or of the selection): every row of `allen_image_io.subimage_rows(sid)` -- the two RMA queries of `list_images` (SubImage by each data set of the specimen, and by `specimen_id`), deduplicated by `id` keeping the first -- whose `image_type` is not "Primary", with every field the API returns, verbatim; the usable planes (`plane_table` of `images_frame(rows)`): their count, the failed count, the median width and height; the xy candidates of 16.2 (a) (`xy_projections`); and 16.2 (a)'s darkest rule on them (each fetched whole at `align_downsample`, `darkest`): the candidate Cell 12 would take
- 16.3 (b), the operator test (the specimens of `--check`; default 529878215). Notation: $\mathcal K$ the usable planes of the specimen, $I_k(x, y)$ the grey level of plane $k \in \mathcal K$ at the full-resolution pixel $(x, y)$ (a crop at downsample 0, the JPEG as served), $h$ = `projcheck_half_px` (256 px = 29 um), $s$ = `projcheck_max_shift_px` (3 px), $W = \{(x, y) \in \mathbb Z^2 : x_0 \le x < x_0 + 2h,\ y_0 \le y < y_0 + 2h\}$ the window, whose corner is `place_window`: $x_0 = \min(\max(\lfloor c_x + 1/2 \rfloor - h,\ s),\ X - 2h - s)$, $y_0$ likewise with $c_y$ and $Y$, where $(c_x, c_y)$ is the centre and $X$, $Y$ the smallest width and height among the planes' medians and the candidates (so that every crop below lies inside every image; $X < 2(h + s)$ or $Y < 2(h + s)$ raises ValueError). The centre: the SWC's soma -- the mean full-res position of its type-1 rows with parent -1 (else of every type-1 row; none raises ValueError naming `--center`) under Block 0's transform (`swc_to_full_px`) with the cell's alignment record (16.2 (e), `full_transform`) when one is given or found under `--survey-dir`, otherwise with no shift and no flip, as for 529878215 -- or a pixel given as `--center X,Y`; the record says which (`center_source`). The operators, for every $(x, y) \in W$: $O_{\min}(x, y) = \min_{k \in \mathcal K} I_k(x, y)$, $O_{\max} = \max_{k \in \mathcal K} I_k$, $O_{\rm mean} = |\mathcal K|^{-1}\sum_{k \in \mathcal K} I_k$, $O_{\rm median}$ = the median over $k \in \mathcal K$ (`plane_operators`; a section missing from the stack is `invalid` in `fetch_zblock` and excluded; fewer than 2 valid planes raises ValueError). For each candidate projection $P$ -- its crop at downsample 0 over $W$ widened by $s$ px on every side -- and each operator $o$, for every integer offset $(a, b)$ with $|a| \le s$, $|b| \le s$: $r_o(a, b)$ = the Pearson correlation over $(x, y) \in W$ of $P(x + a, y + b)$ with $O_o(x, y)$, NaN when either is constant over $W$; $(a^*_o, b^*_o) = \arg\max_{a, b} r_o(a, b)$ over the finite values, the first maximum in the order $b = -s..s$, then $a = -s..s$; $r^*_o = r_o(a^*_o, b^*_o)$; the least-squares line $P(x + a^*_o, y + b^*_o) \approx \beta_{0,o} + \beta_{1,o}\,O_o(x, y)$ over $W$, and the RMS of its residual (grey levels) (`shift_match`). The offset is the projection's pixel minus the planes' pixel holding the same content. The verdict (`classify`): $o^\dagger = \arg\max_o r^*_o$ over the operators with a finite $r^*_o$, $o^\ddagger$ the next, the lead $m = r^*_{o^\dagger} - r^*_{o^\ddagger}$ and the ratio $q = (1 - r^{*2}_{o^\ddagger}) / (1 - r^{*2}_{o^\dagger})$ -- how many times the best line's unexplained variance the next operator's line leaves ($q = (\mathrm{RMS}_{o^\ddagger}/\mathrm{RMS}_{o^\dagger})^2$ on a common crop; $+\infty$ when $r^*_{o^\dagger} = 1$ or one operator is finite); `matches <o>` when $r^*_{o^\dagger} \ge$ `projcheck_min_r` (0.9) and $q \ge$ `projcheck_min_ratio` (4: the next line's residual RMS at least twice the best's), otherwise `unclear: best <o> r <r>, ratio <q>`; no finite $r^*$: `unclear: no finite r`. The ratio decides, not the lead [corrected 2026-10-27 with the smoke test: the draft's rule, $m \ge$ 0.05, called the exact minimum `unclear` -- on the synthetic stacks the planes' mean correlates with their minimum at $r^*$ 0.95-0.97, while its line leaves 8-13 gl RMS against the minimum's 2.3-2.6 gl with JPEG on both sides, $q$ 12-31 (9 stacks: 3 shapes x 3 seeds) **[run, sandbox]**]. Cost: one crop of $2h \times 2h$ px per usable plane (about 520 for 529878215) and one per candidate, all through the crop cache
- 16.3 (c), outputs (`--out-dir`, in Cell 11b `survey/projections/`): `projections_<sid>.json` -- `specimen_id`, `subimages` (the non-Primary rows, verbatim), `planes` {`n_usable`, `n_failed`, `width_px`, `height_px`}, `candidates` (their ids), `darkest` {`id`, `downsample`, `medians` {id: grey level}}, and with the operator test `window` {`left`, `top`, `half_px`, `max_shift_px`, `center_x`, `center_y`, `center_source`}, `n_planes_used`, `checks` [{`id`, `image_type`, `by_operator` {o: {`r`, `shift_x_px`, `shift_y_px`, `slope`, `intercept`, `rms_gl`}}, `best`, `margin`, `ratio`, `verdict`}] (empty, with a `note`, when the cell has no xy candidate), `seconds` (an infinite ratio is written as null); `projection_check_<sid>.png` (one row per candidate: the projection over $W$ at the best operator's offset, then $O_{\min}$, $O_{\rm mean}$, $O_{\rm median}$, $O_{\max}$, each titled with its $r^*$ and offset, one grey scale for the row); `projection_types.csv`, one row per non-Primary SubImage of every specimen listed: `specimen_id`, `id`, `image_type`, `width`, `height`, `xy_candidate`, `darkest`, `best`, `r`, `shift_x_px`, `shift_y_px`, `verdict` (the last five empty without the operator test)
- Public API (16.3): `projection_check.OPERATORS` = ("min", "mean", "median", "max"); `projection_check.plane_operators(stack, valid=None) -> {op: (H, W) float64}`; `projection_check.shift_match(P_big, O, max_shift) -> dict` (`r`, `shift_x_px`, `shift_y_px`, `slope`, `intercept`, `rms_gl`); `projection_check.classify(by_op, min_r, min_ratio) -> (best, margin, ratio, verdict)`; `projection_check.check_projection(P_big, ops, max_shift, min_r, min_ratio) -> dict` (`by_operator`, `best`, `margin`, `ratio`, `verdict`); `projection_check.place_window(cx, cy, half, margin, width, height) -> (left, top)`; `projection_check.aligned_crop(P_big, a, b, max_shift) -> ndarray`; `projection_check.soma_center(swc, rho0, shift_full_px=(0, 0), flip_y_full_h=None) -> (cx, cy)`; `allen_image_io.subimage_rows(specimen_id) -> list of dict`; `allen_image_io.images_frame(rows) -> DataFrame` (`list_images(sid)` = `images_frame(subimage_rows(sid))`); `survey_figures.projection_check_figure(rec, ops, crops) -> Figure`; `scripts/projection_check.py [--specimens IDS] [--selection SEL.csv] [--check IDS|none] --cache-dir C --out-dir OUT [--center soma|X,Y] [--survey-dir S] [--config-json J]`
- Parameters (16.3; `SurveyConfig`, in no signature): `projcheck_half_px` 256, `projcheck_max_shift_px` 3, `projcheck_min_r` 0.9, `projcheck_min_ratio` 4.0 -- all PROVISIONAL
- Library calls relied on (16.3): `allen_image_io.api_query`, `plane_table`, `fetch_zblock`, `fetch_crop`, `fetch_whole`, `fetch_swc`; `allen_image_align.swc_to_full_px`; `allen_image_plot.read_swc`; numpy (`min`, `max`, `mean`, `median` along the plane axis; the correlation and the line from centred moments); matplotlib (`imshow`)
- Custom code (16.3): the offset search with a correlation per offset (Custom: `skimage.registration.phase_cross_correlation` returns one shift per pair and no correlation, `scipy.signal.correlate2d` unnormalised products; the normalisation per window is what makes $r$ comparable across operators; checked: both, and `numpy.corrcoef`, the test's reference)
- Test oracles (16.3, `tests/smoke/test_smoke_projection_check.py`): (a) known answer: a synthetic stack -- dark tubes each in focus at its own plane and blurred away from it, on a bright noisy field -- and its exact pixelwise minimum and maximum, placed in images 2 px larger than the planes with the content offset by (+1, +1) px (Allen's projections are 2 px larger than its planes); `check_projection` returns `matches min` at offset (+1, +1) with $r^* \ge$ 0.999, a zero residual and $q \ge 10^6$ for the first and `matches max` for the second; content at (+2, 0) is reported at (+2, 0); with every image served as a JPEG (Pillow, quality 85), still `matches min` at (+1, +1) with $r^* \ge$ 0.99, $q \ge$ 8 and the residual RMS $\le$ 4 gl, while the lead in $r$ alone stays below 0.05; (b) reference: $r^*$ equals `numpy.corrcoef` on the shifted crop, the line `numpy.polyfit`, the RMS the residual's; `plane_operators` equals the operators written out plane by plane; `aligned_crop` at (+2, 0) the window shifted 2 px along x; `soma_center` the soma's pixel under no shift and under a shift with a flip; (c) invariants: $\beta_0 + \beta_1 P$ ($\beta_1 > 0$) leaves $r^*$ and the offset unchanged; permuting the planes leaves the operators unchanged; (d) contract: the record's keys and JSON round trip; `images_frame` on canned rows equals the table `list_images` built before the split; the script end to end on a synthetic source -- a field the code does not know is listed verbatim, the CSV's rows, the figure; (e) determinism: two runs give identical JSON; (f) edge cases: fewer than 2 valid planes refused; a missing (white) section excluded through `valid` (the maximum would be 255 everywhere otherwise); a failed plane counted and not used; a constant projection gives NaN and `unclear`; a window centred at the edge moved inside, an image smaller than the window refused; `--check none` lists only; a specimen without an xy candidate listed, not failed; no soma without `--center` refused
- Data flow (16.3): reads what 16.2 (a) reads; its report settles 16.2 (a)'s rule (D-090.1); feeds nothing automatically
- Status (16.3): drafted 2026-10-27; implemented 2026-10-27 (`analysis/projection_check.py`, `allen_image_io.subimage_rows`/`images_frame`, `survey_figures.projection_check_figure`, `SurveyConfig.projcheck_*`, `scripts/projection_check.py`, notebook Cell 11b and runbook v29; the estimator and simulation hashes unchanged). `test_smoke_projection_check.py` 6 pass, 1 skip (`test_convergence`: no discretisation), 3 s; 16 of 16 mutants each failed a check (the mean, `valid`, the offset's sign and axes, the residual, the slope, the ratio's direction, the verdict on the lead, a constant crop, the window's two clips, `aligned_crop`'s axes, the soma rows, the darkest flag, the failed count, the first of a repeated id) **[run, sandbox]**. Not run against Allen's server (Colab Cell 11b). Smoke-tested

### Block 17: S3a, the real-cell survey -- the node sample, the measurement, the coverage of the phantoms' band, the diameters by layer and class (S3a of the 2026-10-20 plan; D-059.1, D-065.1, D-085.4-6, D-087)

- Modules: `src/allen_diameter/analysis/s3_nodes.py` (the node sample), `src/allen_diameter/analysis/survey.py` (`measure_nodes` gains `whole_stretches`), `src/allen_diameter/analysis/coverage.py` (the per-bin coverage, the pass rule, the diameter tables), `src/allen_diameter/plotting/survey_figures.py` (the coverage and diameter figures), `src/allen_diameter/config.py` (`SurveyConfig`'s `nodes_*` and `coverage_*` fields, in no signature), `scripts/survey_cells.py` (the step `nodes`; `pool --what coverage`), notebook Cell 13 and the runbook. No thick-node check (D-085.6: the renderer is not the final one)
- Notation: for a node $i$ of cell $c$: $r_{{\rm A},i}$ Allen's radius (um), $D_{{\rm A},i} = 2 r_{{\rm A},i}$; $\hat d_i$, $\hat\mu_i$ (/um), $\varphi_i$ and $\hat\alpha_i$ = `alpha_hat` the fitted values of Block 13's chain at $k^{\rm it}$ (the per-node CSV's `d_hat_um`, `mu_hat_per_um`, `phi_rad`, `alpha_hat`), $\hat\mu_i\hat d_i$ = `mu_d_hat`; $\mathcal S$ the deliverable's selection (`in_S`); $[\mu_{\min}, \mu_{\max}]$ = `phantom.mu_range_per_um` (0.3, 3 /um, D-065.1)
- 17.1, the node sample (D-085.4; per cell): `survey.nodes_mode` = `stratified` (default, the assistant's, confirmed by the user, D-089) or `all` [user: both, exposed]. `all`: every dendrite node (SWC types `acquisition.dendrite_swc_types`). `stratified`: the bins $\mathcal B_b = [e_b, e_{b+1})$ of $D_{\rm A}$ with edges (0, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 1.2, 2, $\infty$) um (`nodes_allen_bins_um` = the inner edges (0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 1.2, 2.0)) [D-085.4]; in bin $b$ the cell's dendrite nodes with $D_{\rm A}$ in it, grouped by stretch (`cell.stretches`); the stretches in the order of a permutation and each stretch's nodes in the order of a permutation, both from `numpy.random.default_rng([selection_seed, specimen_id, b])`; nodes taken round-robin over the stretches -- the first of every stretch in turn, then the second, and so on -- until $N$ = `nodes_per_bin` (20) [D-085.4] or the bin is exhausted. A node with a non-finite or negative radius belongs to no bin. Output: the node ids, per bin the count available and taken
- 17.2, the measurement (per cell; D-065.1): Block 13's `measure_node` on each sampled node -- with its stretch's geometry and the cell's scene, the cell's transform of 16.2 (`cell_alignment.full_transform`), no registration -- through `survey.measure_nodes(..., only=ids, whole_stretches=False)` (with `whole_stretches=True`, the default, every node of a stretch holding an id is measured, as before); the provider `run_cell.real_provider` over the cell's planes (`plane_table` of its SubImages). Per node the CSV row of Block 8 (`survey.pilot_row`) and the background statistics (`survey.background_stats`: median, robust SD, clipped SD, unmasked share, for S3b's camera, D-085.9). Written: `<cell>/nodes/pilot_<sid>.csv` and `pilot_summary_<sid>.json` (`survey.summarize`, `background_summary`, the mode, the sample's counts per bin, the transform)
- 17.3, the coverage (pooled over the cells with a `nodes` marker; D-065.1, D-085.5): the nodes in $\mathcal S$ with finite $\hat d_i > 0$, $\hat\mu_i > 0$; the bins of $\hat d$ with edges `coverage_dhat_bins_um` = (0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 1.2, 2.0, 4.0) um (PROVISIONAL; the phantoms' range [0.2, 4]) plus the open bins below the first and from the last edge, each $[e, e')$. Per bin: $n$ nodes, $n$ cells; the 5, 25, 50, 75, 95 % quantiles of $\ln(\hat\mu\hat d)$ and of $\ln\hat\alpha$ (numpy `quantile`, linear); the analytic band of D-065.1, $\ln(\hat\mu_i\hat d_i) \in [\ln\mu_{\min} + \ln\hat d_i, \ln\mu_{\max} + \ln\hat d_i]$ at the node's own $\hat d_i$ (standing in for the phantom's true $d$), which holds exactly when $\mu_{\min} \le \hat\mu_i \le \mu_{\max}$: the shares inside, below and above; with phantom rows (optional; Block 6's replicate CSVs, rows in $\mathcal S$ with finite $\hat d$, $\hat\mu$), the phantoms' band per bin = the `coverage_phantom_quantiles` (0.005, 0.995; PROVISIONAL) quantiles of their $\ln(\hat\mu\hat d)$ in the same bin of $\hat d$, and the share of real nodes inside it (NaN, `status_phantom` = `no_phantoms`, where the bin holds fewer than 20 phantom rows). The pass rule (D-085.5, fixed before the run) [user]: every bin with at least `coverage_min_nodes` (20) nodes has a share inside of at least `coverage_pass_share` (0.95); a bin with fewer is `too_few`, not failing; the share is the analytic band's, and the phantoms' band's verdict is reported beside it when rows are given. A failing bin is reported; widening the $\mu$ range stays a later decision (D-065.1)
- 17.4, the diameters by layer and class (D-085.5, D-087): per (class, layer), per class and per layer: $n$ nodes, $n$ cells, the 5, 25, 50, 75, 95 % quantiles of $\hat d$ (in $\mathcal S$; fitted, not corrected -- no table yet) and of $D_{\rm A}$ (every measured node)
- 17.5, the figures: `coverage_mud.png` -- every node's $(\ln\hat d, \ln\hat\mu\hat d)$, coloured by class, the analytic band's edges and the bin edges, the phantoms' band per bin when given; `coverage_share.png` -- the share inside per bin with its $n$, the 0.95 line, bins with fewer than 20 nodes hatched; `coverage_alpha.png` -- $\ln\hat\alpha$ per bin (boxes at the quantiles), the analytic lower edge $\ln\mu_{\min} + \ln\hat d$ at the bin's geometric centre (the upper edge is unbounded as $\varphi \to 90°$); `diameters.png` -- per layer, $\hat d$ and $D_{\rm A}$ by class
- Outputs of `pool --what coverage`: `<out>/s3a/coverage.csv` (one row per bin), `coverage.json` (the bins, the verdict, the rule, the per-cell node counts, the counts outside $\mathcal S$ by reason), `diameters.csv`, the figures
- Public API: `s3_nodes.stratified_nodes(swc, cfg, specimen_id) -> (ids, counts)`; `s3_nodes.all_nodes(swc, cfg) -> ids`; `s3_nodes.sample_nodes(swc, cfg, specimen_id) -> (ids, counts)` (by `nodes_mode`); `survey.measure_nodes(swc, provider, cfg, transform=None, regs=None, only=None, log=None, whole_stretches=True)`; `coverage.node_table(rows_by_cell) -> list of dict` (the pooled rows with `specimen_id`, `neuron_class`, `layer`); `coverage.coverage_table(nodes, cfg, phantom=None) -> list of dict`; `coverage.verdict(bins, cfg) -> dict`; `coverage.diameter_table(nodes) -> list of dict`; `coverage.bin_edges(cfg) -> [(label, lo, hi)]`; `coverage.outside_counts(nodes) -> dict`; `survey_cells.read_phantom_rows(paths) -> dict of arrays` (the replicate CSVs through `table_io.read_columns`: `d_hat`, `mud` of the rows in $\mathcal S$); `survey_figures.coverage_figures(nodes, bins, cfg, phantom=None) -> {name: Figure}`, `survey_figures.diameter_figure(nodes) -> Figure`; `scripts/survey_cells.py run --steps align,nodes`, `pool --out-dir OUT --what coverage [--selection SEL.csv] [--phantom-rows CSV ...] [--config-json]`
- Parameters (`SurveyConfig`, in no signature): `nodes_mode` "stratified" (the default confirmed by the user, D-089) (the modes D-085.4), `nodes_per_bin` 20 [D-085.4], `nodes_allen_bins_um` (0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 1.2, 2.0) [D-085.4], `coverage_dhat_bins_um` (0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 1.2, 2.0, 4.0) PROVISIONAL, `coverage_pass_share` 0.95 [D-085.5], `coverage_min_nodes` 20 [D-085.5], `coverage_phantom_quantiles` (0.005, 0.995) PROVISIONAL
- Library calls relied on: Block 13 (`measure_node` through `survey.measure_nodes`), Block 8 (`survey.pilot_row`, `summarize`, `background_stats`, `background_summary`), `run_cell.real_provider`, `table_io.write_rows`, `read_rows`, `read_columns`; numpy (`quantile`, `random.default_rng`, `digitize`); matplotlib
- Custom code: the round-robin stratified sample; the per-bin coverage table (counts and shares; no library)
- Test oracles (`tests/smoke/test_smoke_s3a.py`): (a) known answer: on a synthetic SWC with stretches of known radii, the stratified sample takes min($N$, available) per bin and no stretch gives a second node to a bin before every stretch with nodes in that bin has given one; `all` gives every dendrite node; on hand-made node rows, the shares, quantiles, $n$ cells and the verdict come out as computed by hand (a bin of 19 nodes all outside: `too_few`; 20 nodes with 1 outside: share 0.95, passes; 2 outside: fails); (b) reference: the analytic share equals the share computed from the $\ln(\hat\mu\hat d)$ bounds directly; the quantiles equal `numpy.quantile`; the phantoms' band equals `numpy.quantile` of their rows in the bin; (c) invariants: the rows' order and nodes outside $\mathcal S$ change nothing; (d) contract: the CSV and JSON columns and keys, the figures written; `measure_nodes(..., whole_stretches=False)` measures exactly the ids given, `True` the whole stretches as before; (e) determinism: the sample twice with the seed, another seed changes a bin with more than $N$ candidates; (f) edge cases: an empty bin, a node with a NaN radius, a cell without nodes in $\mathcal S$; the step `nodes` end to end on the synthetic cell of `fixtures_cell.py` served through `fetch_zblock` (every sampled node measured, the CSV and summary written, the background statistics present), and `pool --what coverage` over it
- Data flow: consumes 16.1 (the cells, their class and layer), 16.2 (the transform, the set-aside verdict), Blocks 8 and 13; feeds S3b (the background statistics for the camera, D-085.9), S3d (the coverage verdict for the $\mu$ range), and the user's decision on the $\mu$ range (D-065.1)
- Confirmed: D-059.1, D-065.1, D-085.4-6, D-087 (the user); the defaults and readings marked PROVISIONAL are the assistant's
- Status: drafted 2026-10-26; implemented 2026-10-26 (`analysis/s3_nodes.py`, `analysis/coverage.py`, `survey.measure_nodes(whole_stretches=)`, the figures, `SurveyConfig.nodes_*`/`coverage_*`, the step `nodes` and `pool --what coverage`, the source's `planes`, notebook Cell 13 and runbook v26; the estimator and simulation hashes unchanged). `test_smoke_s3a.py` 6 pass, 1 skip (`test_convergence`), 26 s: the sample equals an independent re-implementation and spreads round-robin; the hand-made coverage, shares, quantiles and pass rule as computed by hand; the step `nodes` end to end on the synthetic cell (6 of 6 nodes measured through `fetch_zblock`, the background columns written) and the pooled coverage over it; 16 of 16 mutants each failed a check (two after checks were added for the open bin above 4 um and the CSV's `in_S` strings) **[run, sandbox]**. The figures read on a synthetic pooled set of 15 cells (`coverage_mud`, `coverage_share`, `coverage_alpha`, `diameters`). Not run on Allen's cells (Colab Cell 13): the shares, the verdict and the time per cell unknown until then. Smoke-tested

### Block 18: S3b, the simulator's inputs across cells -- $d_{\max}$, the node jitter, the camera (S3b of the 2026-10-20 plan; D-065.2-3, D-085.8-9, D-086)

- Modules: `src/allen_diameter/analysis/s3_inputs.py` (the pooled suggestions: $d_{\max}$, the jitter SDs, the camera settings; arrays and records in, records out), `src/allen_diameter/plotting/survey_figures.py` (`inputs_figure`), `scripts/survey_cells.py` (the steps `radius`, `registration`, `camera`; `pool --what inputs`), notebook Cell 14 and the runbook. Every value is a suggestion: the production configuration takes it only when the user writes it into Cell 8's `DECIDED` (D-065)
- 18.1, `radius` (per cell; SWC only, D-065.2): `allen_radius_distribution.collect([swc], types=(3, 4))` -- per dendrite node its id, type and $D_{{\rm A},i} = 2 r_{{\rm A},i}$, and the file's summary (the script's percentiles, mean, shares above thresholds) -- written to `<cell>/radius/radius_nodes_<sid>.csv` and `radius_summary_<sid>.csv`; the step's summary: $n$, the cell's maximum $D_{\rm A}$ and the node that holds it, p99
- 18.2, `registration` (per cell; D-085.8): the stretches suggested by `list_stretches.run(swc, ..., per_type=3, min_nodes=10)` (one node per stretch, spread over path distance, basal and apical: the rule of Cell 3a) [D-085.8: "the nodes of a cell from list_stretches' suggestion"], then `registration_survey.run` on those nodes with the cell's transform (16.2) -- per node the verdict, the lateral offset $s^*$ (`lateral_offset_um`) and the depth offset $\Delta z^*$ (`z_offset_um`) -- into `<cell>/registration/` (`stretches_<sid>.csv/.json`, `registration_<sid>.json`, `registration_summary_<sid>.json`)
- 18.3, `camera` (per cell; D-085.9; after `nodes`): `camera_calibration.run(cache_dir, <cell>/camera, sid, pilot_summary=<cell>/nodes/pilot_summary_<sid>.json)` -- the suggested `background_B_gl` (the median of the nodes' background medians), `noise_sd_gl` (matched through the camera chain to the median clipped background SD; an upper bound) and the JPEG table set (the most common in the crop cache, which every cell shares)
- 18.4, the pooled suggestions (`pool --what inputs`; `s3_inputs`):
  - $d_{\max}$ (D-086) [user]: $\max_c \max_{i \in \mathcal N_c} D_{{\rm A},i}$ over the cells with a `radius` marker -- the SWC types 3, 4 -- with the cell and the node that set it; beside it, as comparisons, the pooled p99 and p99.5 of $D_{\rm A}$ over every node and each cell's maximum
  - the jitter (D-085.8) [user]: over the ON nodes (`analysis.registration.registration_category` = ON) of every cell with a `registration` marker, pooled: `jitter_xy_um` = $1.4826\,\mathrm{median}|s^* - \mathrm{median}(s^*)|$, `jitter_z_um` the same of $\Delta z^*$; per cell the same statistics, the medians and the count of ON nodes; a cell whose $|\mathrm{median}(\Delta z^*)|$ exceeds `inputs_z0_flag_um` (1.0 um, PROVISIONAL) is flagged `z0` -- its plane origin $z_0$ (16.2, `align_z0_um`) may be off
  - the camera (D-085.9) [user]: over the cells with a `camera` marker, the most common JPEG table set (by the tables' identity; its share of the cells), the median of `background_B_gl` and the median of `noise_sd_gl` (an upper bound, as for one cell)
  - Output: `<out>/s3b/inputs.json` (the suggestions under `suggested`, each with its rule and its decision, the per-cell table, the comparisons), `inputs_per_cell.csv`, `inputs.png`
- 18.5, the figure (`survey_figures.inputs_figure`): the pooled distribution of $D_{\rm A}$ (log axis) with $d_{\max}$, p99 and p99.5 marked and the cell maxima as ticks; the pooled $s^*$ and $\Delta z^*$ of the ON nodes with $\pm$ the robust SD; per cell the background and the noise SD with their medians
- Public API: `s3_inputs.d_max(cells) -> dict` (cells: [{`specimen_id`, `node_ids`, `D_allen`}]); `s3_inputs.jitter(cells, z0_flag_um) -> dict` (cells: [{`specimen_id`, `s_star`, `dz_star`}] of ON nodes); `s3_inputs.camera(cells) -> dict` (cells: [{`specimen_id`, `background_B_gl`, `noise_sd_gl`, `jpeg_qtables`}]); `s3_inputs.robust_sd(x) -> float`; `survey_figures.inputs_figure(...) -> Figure`; `scripts/survey_cells.py run --steps align,nodes,radius,registration,camera`, `pool --what inputs`
- Parameters: `SurveyConfig.inputs_z0_flag_um` 1.0 (the assistant's, confirmed by the user, D-089), in no signature; the rest from D-085.8-9, D-086
- Library calls relied on: `allen_radius_distribution.collect`, `list_stretches.run`, `registration_survey.run` (Block 0's `registration_check`), `camera_calibration.run` (Block 4's chain, `jpeg_tables`); numpy (`percentile`, `median`, `quantile`)
- Custom code: none numerical beyond medians and MADs
- Test oracles (`tests/smoke/test_smoke_s3b.py`): (a) known answer: $d_{\max}$ of three cells' hand-made diameters is their largest, with its cell and node; the pooled robust SD of hand-made offsets equals $1.4826\times$ the MAD by hand; the most common table set among three cells, the medians of B and noise by hand; a cell with median $\Delta z^*$ = 1.5 um flagged `z0`; (b) reference: `robust_sd` against `scipy.stats.median_abs_deviation(scale="normal")`; the pooled p99 and p99.5 against `numpy.percentile`; (c) invariants: the cells' order changes nothing; non-ON nodes do not enter the jitter; (d) contract: `inputs.json`'s keys, the CSV, the figure; (e) determinism: two pools give identical files; (f) edge cases: no ON node in a cell (its SDs NaN, the pooled from the others); no cell with a marker (the suggestion absent and said so); the steps `radius`, `registration` and `camera` end to end on the synthetic cell of `fixtures_cell.py` (the registration's verdict on its one stretch, the camera's B from the nodes' background), then `pool --what inputs` over it
- Data flow: consumes 16.2 (the transform), 17 (the background statistics), Block 0, Block 4's camera chain; feeds Cell 8 (the user's `DECIDED`) and S3d
- Confirmed: D-065.2-3, D-085.8-9, D-086 (the user); `inputs_z0_flag_um` (confirmed by the user, D-089) and the readings marked PROVISIONAL are the assistant's
- Status: drafted 2026-10-26; implemented 2026-10-26 (`analysis/s3_inputs.py`, `survey_figures.inputs_figure`, the steps `radius`, `registration`, `camera`, `pool --what inputs`, `SurveyConfig.registration_per_type`, `registration_min_nodes`, `inputs_z0_flag_um`, notebook Cell 14 and runbook v27; the estimator and simulation hashes unchanged). `test_smoke_s3b.py` 6 pass, 1 skip, 21 s: the three steps end to end on the synthetic cell (the registration ON, B from the nodes' background), the pool over it and over hand-written folders (d_max the largest 2r, not a percentile; the jitter from the ON nodes only; the camera medians); 13 of 13 mutants each failed a check (one after a check of the step `radius` on varied radii was added) **[run, sandbox]**. The figure read on the synthetic cell. Not run on Allen's cells (Colab Cell 14). Smoke-tested

### Block 19: S3c, one defocus kernel calibrated on nodes pooled across cells -- 10 to 30 nodes per set, the per-cell scans, the pooled fit (S3c of the 2026-10-20 plan; D-085.10 superseded by D-088)

- Modules: `src/allen_diameter/analysis/calibration.py` (`scan_cell` gains a scan order and a cap per set), `src/allen_diameter/analysis/calibration_pool.py` (the pooled set: qualified node ids, the round-robin over the cells, the sufficiency), `src/allen_diameter/config.py` (`CalibrationConfig.calib_min_nodes_per_set`, `calib_max_nodes_per_set`, `calib_order_seed`; `SurveyConfig.calib_scans_per_cell`; in no signature), `scripts/calibrate_kernel.py` (`real` with the order and the cap; `fit` and `far-fit` with the minimum), `scripts/survey_cells.py` (the step `calibration`; `pool --what calibration`; 19.5: the CSV), `src/allen_diameter/analysis/calibration_report.py` (19.5, added 2026-10-27: `law_points`, `pooled_sigma_law`, `kernel_law_by_cell`), `src/allen_diameter/plotting/calibration_figures.py` (19.5: the two figures; `write_all(id_map=)`), notebook Cells 7 and 15 and the runbook; 19.5 also adds `calibration_pool.cell_of` and the id map of `calibrate_kernel.py figures`
- 19.1, the scan order and the cap (D-088.3; Blocks 10 and 15): `calibration.scan_cell(..., order_rng=None, max_per_set=None)` -- with `order_rng`, the candidate nodes are taken in the order of `order_rng.permutation` over them (in the SWC's order before it); with `max_per_set`, a candidate whose set (by Allen's diameter, `calibration_set`, known before any fetch) already holds `max_per_set` scanned nodes is skipped without being measured, with the reason `set_full`. Without both arguments, `scan_cell` is unchanged. `calibrate_kernel.py real` (Cell 7, one cell) passes `order_rng = default_rng([calib_order_seed, specimen])` and `max_per_set = calib_max_nodes_per_set` (30) [user, D-088.3]
- 19.2, the minimum (D-088.3) [user]: `calib_min_nodes_per_set` = 10. `fit`: the near-field fit runs on any number of nodes from 2 (as before); its file records `sufficient` = ($J_{\rm near} \ge 10$), `n_nodes`, `min_nodes`, and the report prints `INSUFFICIENT: J near-field nodes < 10 (D-088.3): a diagnostic, not the production kernel's table` when it is not. `far-fit`: the same for the far nodes kept by the plane selection ($J_{\rm far}$, Block 15.3); the production kernel (the near table continued with $\hat\gamma$) is written only when both sets are sufficient, otherwise `no production kernel: insufficient ...` (the assistant's rule, PROVISIONAL: below the minimum the fits are diagnostics)
- 19.3, the per-cell step `calibration` (S3c; after `nodes`): the cell's candidates = the nodes of its per-node CSV (Block 17) that can be calibration nodes of either set (`calibrate_kernel.candidates_from_rows`: in $\mathcal S$, flat, faint, Allen's diameter in a set); `scan_cell` with the cell's transform, `order_rng = default_rng([calib_order_seed, specimen_id])` and `max_per_set` = `survey.calib_scans_per_cell` (5, the assistant's, confirmed by the user, D-089: the per-cell quota that keeps the far scans' cost bounded while spreading the pool over the cells); `<cell>/calibration/scans_<sid>.json` (the scans file of Block 10/15, `meta.nodes` with every candidate's reasons) -- the step's summary: the candidates, the near and far scans, the reasons' counts
- 19.4, the pooled calibration (`pool --what calibration`; `calibration_pool`) [D-088.1]: the scans of every cell with a `calibration` marker; each scan's node id qualified as $q = s \cdot 10^6 + n$ ($s$ the specimen id, $n$ the SWC node id; two cells can share a node id and the fits carry one nuisance parameter per node), the map recorded; per set, the cells in increasing specimen id and each cell's scans in its file's order (the seeded scan order), taken round-robin over the cells -- each cell's first, then each one's second -- up to `calib_max_nodes_per_set` (30); the pooled scans `<out>/s3c/scans_pooled.json` (meta: the per-set and per-cell counts, the map, `sufficient` per set); then Block 10's `fit` and Block 15's `far-fit` and `figures` on it (`calibrate_kernel.run_fit`, `run_far_fit`, `run_figures`) -> `s3c/calibration_pooled.json`, `s3c/far_fit_pooled.json`, `s3c/figures/`. One kernel, one table (D-088.1)
- 19.5, the pooled law by cell (D-090.2: the user, 2026-10-27, "plot the near and far field points toghether but with a cell base color code so that we can evaluate discrepancies among cells"): `calibration_report.law_points(scans, cfg, calib, far_fit)` -- the points of `kernel_law_inputs` (Block 10.F) with their node: per plane $k$ of node $j$ with a finite width and a known $\hat c_j$, $(|\hat\delta_{j,k}|,\ \hat\sigma_{j,k},\ \mathrm{side},\ \mathrm{used},\ \mathrm{set},\ j)$ with $\hat\sigma_{j,k} = (\hat\omega_{j,k} - \hat c_j + \sigma_{\rm r}(0)^2)^{1/2}$ for the near set and $(\hat\omega_{j,k} - \hat c_j)^{1/2}$ for the far set; `kernel_law_inputs` returns the same points as before (the first five fields). The cell of node $q$ (`calibration_pool.cell_of(q, id_map)`): the specimen the pooled file's map (`meta.id_map`, Block 19.4) gives, else $\lfloor q / 10^6 \rfloor$ for $q \ge 10^6$, else none (one cell: Cell 7's files). The pooled law (`pooled_sigma_law(calib, far_fit)`), for $\delta \ge 0$: $\sigma_{\rm law}(\delta)$ = the calibration file's table (`kernel.kernel_table_delta_um`, `kernel_table_sigma_um`), linear between its points, for $\delta \le \delta_{\rm T}$, its last point (the anchor's 0.84 um); for $\delta > \delta_{\rm T}$ the far line $\hat\sigma_0 + \hat\gamma\,(\delta - \delta_0)$ with the calibration's anchor $(\delta_0, \hat\sigma_0)$ and the far fit's $\hat\gamma$ when the far fit exists, NaN otherwise. The residual of a point: $e_{j,k} = \hat\sigma_{j,k} - \sigma_{\rm law}(|\hat\delta_{j,k}|)$ (um). `calibration_report.kernel_law_by_cell(scans, cfg, calib, far_fit, id_map=None)` -> None without a near fit, else {`points` [{`cell`, `node`, `node_id` (the qualified id), `set`, `abs_delta_um`, `sigma_um`, `side`, `used`, `residual_um`}], `cells` (increasing specimen id, a cell of none last), `summary`, `table`, `line`, `anchor`}; `summary` holds one row per cell and set with a point: `specimen_id` (empty for none), `set`, `n_nodes` (nodes with a used point of finite residual), `n_points_used` (used, finite residual), `n_points`, `median_residual_um`, `q25_residual_um`, `q75_residual_um`, `median_abs_delta_um` (over the used points of finite residual; NaN without one). The figures (`plotting/calibration_figures`), drawn only when the points span 2 cells or more: `kernel_law_by_cell_figure` -- both sets' points against $|\hat\delta|$ with the pooled table and far line in ink: with at most 3 cells one panel, colour by cell in the reference palette's first three categorical slots (blue, orange, aqua: the most a scatter keeps apart for colour-blind readers, dataviz skill), marker by set (near a circle, far a triangle), hollow when the fit did not use the point; with more cells one small panel per cell, its points in the first slot's blue over every cell's used points in light grey, shared axes; `kernel_law_residuals_by_cell_figure` -- the residuals, one row per cell labelled by its specimen, near circles above the row's line and far triangles below, each cell's median per set as an ink bar, zero as a line, colour by cell as in the first figure with at most 3 cells, ink otherwise. `write_all(..., id_map=None)` writes them as `kernel_law_by_cell.png` and `kernel_law_residuals_by_cell.png`; `calibrate_kernel.py figures` passes the scans files' `meta.id_map`; `pool --what calibration` also writes `s3c/kernel_law_by_cell.csv` (`summary`). Not built: a per-cell fit of the kernel and a bootstrap over cells (D-088's open points; D-090.2 asks for the figure)
- Public API: `calibration.scan_cell(swc, provider, cfg, transform=None, regs=None, candidates=None, log=None, keep_images=False, order_rng=None, max_per_set=None)`; `calibration_pool.qualify(scans, specimen_id) -> list of PlaneScan`; `calibration_pool.pool_scans(per_cell, cfg) -> (scans, meta)` (per_cell: [(specimen_id, scans)]); `calibration_pool.sufficiency(n_nodes, cfg) -> dict`; `calibration_pool.cell_of(node_id, id_map=None) -> (specimen or None, node)`; `calibration_report.law_points(scans, cfg, calib=None, far_fit=None) -> list`; `calibration_report.pooled_sigma_law(calib, far_fit=None) -> callable or None`; `calibration_report.kernel_law_by_cell(scans, cfg, calib=None, far_fit=None, id_map=None) -> dict or None`; `calibration_figures.kernel_law_by_cell_figure(kc) -> Figure`; `calibration_figures.kernel_law_residuals_by_cell_figure(kc) -> Figure`; `calibration_figures.write_all(scans, calib, far_fit, patches, cfg, out_dir, every_node=True, dpi=150, id_map=None)`; `scripts/survey_cells.py run --steps ...,calibration`, `pool --what calibration`
- Parameters: `CalibrationConfig.calib_min_nodes_per_set` 10 [user, D-088.3], `calib_max_nodes_per_set` 30 [user, D-088.3], `calib_order_seed` 20261026 (PROVISIONAL); `SurveyConfig.calib_scans_per_cell` 5 (the assistant's, confirmed by the user, D-089); "per type" read as per calibration set (D-088.3; the reading confirmed by the user, D-089); the pilot cell of D-088.2 read as 529878215 (confirmed by the user, D-089)
- Library calls relied on: Blocks 10 and 15 (`scan_node`, `fit_growth`, `kernel_from_growth`, `bootstrap_sigma0`, `far_field.*`), `calibration_io`, numpy (`random.default_rng`)
- Custom code: the round-robin pooling and the id qualification (bookkeeping only)
- Test oracles (`tests/smoke/test_smoke_s3c.py`): (a) known answer: the round-robin pool of three cells' hand-made scans (near: 3, 1, 2 scans; far: 0, 4, 4) with a cap of 4 takes, in order, the first of each cell, then the second of each, ...; the qualified ids $s\cdot 10^6 + n$; `sufficiency` at 9 and 10 nodes; (b) reference: the pooled set equals an independent re-implementation; (c) invariants: the cells' order in the input changes nothing (the pool sorts them); two cells with the same node id stay two nodes; (d) contract: `scan_cell` with `max_per_set` stops each set at the cap, the skipped candidates reasoned `set_full` and never measured (a provider that counts its calls), with `order_rng` the scans in the permutation's order; `fit` and `far-fit` on scans below the minimum write `sufficient` false and no production kernel, at and above it a production kernel (synthetic near and far scans of Blocks 10 and 15); the step `calibration` and `pool --what calibration` end to end on the synthetic cell; (e) determinism: the pool twice gives identical files; (f) edge cases: no scans in a set; a cell without candidates (the step's summary says so, no failure). 19.5 (`tests/smoke/test_smoke_calibration_by_cell.py`): (a) known answer: four cells' exact near scans of one growth, fitted pooled (Block 10's fit recovers $\hat c_j$ and the growth on exact data): every near residual with $|\hat\delta| \le \delta_{\rm T}$ equals $(G_{\rm true}(|\hat\delta|) + \sigma_{\rm r}(0)^2)^{1/2}$ minus the table's linear interpolation at $|\hat\delta|$, to $10^{-6}$ um [corrected 2026-10-27 with the smoke test: the draft's oracle, residuals within the interpolation error of $10^{-3}$ um, did not hold -- the growth is a cubic in $G$ (`growth_interp` cubic) and the table linear in $\sigma$ between the same knots, 0.28 um apart, and they differ by up to 0.075 um between knots on this fixture (its cubic dips below zero near 0.1 um); the residual against the production law carries that difference, the same for every cell]; exact far scans with $\gamma$ = 0.79 for three cells and 0.99 for the fourth, the far fit given by hand with $\hat\gamma$ = 0.79, the true $c_j$ and $s_j$ = 0: every far residual equals $(\gamma_{\rm cell} - 0.79)(|\hat\delta| - \delta_0)$, zero for three cells, and the fourth's summary median equals 0.2 times the median of its used points' $|\hat\delta| - \delta_0$; (b) reference: `cell_of` against the map and the division; $\sigma_{\rm law}$ against `numpy.interp` and the line; the summary's quartiles against `numpy.percentile`; (c) invariants: `kernel_law_inputs`'s points are `law_points` without the node; the scans' order changes no summary row; (d) contract: the summary's keys; `write_all` with two or more cells writes the two by-cell figures, with one cell neither; one panel for 3 cells, one per cell for 4; `pool --what calibration` on four cells' exact scans writes `kernel_law_by_cell.csv` and the figures; (e) determinism: the CSV twice identical; (f) edge cases: an unqualified node (one cell) has no cell; a point beyond the table without a far fit has a NaN residual and is left out of the summary; a set without used points gives NaN medians
- Data flow: consumes 16.2 (the transform), 17 (the per-node CSV: the candidates), Blocks 10 and 15; feeds S3d (the kernel and its sufficiency) and Cell 8 (`renderer.kernel_table_*`, `kernel_continuation_slope`)
- Confirmed: D-088 (the user); the values and rules marked PROVISIONAL are the assistant's

### Block 21: the hybrid absorption renderer -- each slab's absorbed light from the condenser's rays, then the defocus kernel (the roadmap's HR; D-084, D-092; I-004; the renderers document of 2026-10-09, s.3.8-3.9, Eqs. 13-14, on `main`)

Block 20 is S3d, the collector of the production configuration (the plan of 2026-10-20), not drafted yet; S3d waits for this block (D-085.11).

- Modules: `src/allen_diameter/model/hybrid.py` (new: the back lengths, the ray history, one slab's absorbed light); `src/allen_diameter/model/render.py` (the mode `"hybrid"`: `ABSORPTIONS_RENDERED`, the dispatch in `_slabs`, each slab computed once per `render_transmittance` call, `absorbed_fractions(..., directions=)`); `src/allen_diameter/config.py` (`ABSORPTIONS` gains `"hybrid"`; no new field); `checks/hybrid_absorption_check.py` and `checks/hybrid_absorption_check.out` (copied byte-identical from `main`: the theory chat's prototype of 2026-10-09, the reference of the oracles below); `checks/hybrid_2026-10-27/` (the profile surveys of the findings, with their outputs)
- 21.1, the model: section 2.3's (H), then (5-6) unchanged. Inputs: a Block 2 `Tube` (um, rad), $\mu$ (1/um, >= 0), the plane depths (um), a `FineGrid`, `RendererConfig`, `light_direction` (+1 or -1). Output: Block 4's `RenderResult`, unchanged in form
- 21.2, the directions [PROVISIONAL, D-092]: Block 9's quadrature, `ray_world.directions(ray_n_rho, ray_n_psi, ray_na, ray_n_oil)` -- one condenser for the ray world and the hybrid; no field of the hybrid's own, so the simulation hash of every configuration that does not select the hybrid is unchanged (the default's: estimator `0508e7ff12f48728`, simulation `0ea5a6d2ec069c68`; with `absorption = "hybrid"`: estimator unchanged, simulation `5e5b04a2f154e1c8`). The pointwise error of $h$ against 48 x 96 directions, over 400 interior points of a 1 um tube ($\varphi$ = 0, 45, 80 deg; $\mu$ = 0.3, 3, 10 /um): at most 3.4e-3 at 16 x 32, 1.5e-2 at 8 x 16, 7.8e-2 at 4 x 8 **[run, sandbox]**
- 21.3, the slab rule [PROVISIONAL, D-092]: the midpoint $\bar\zeta_j$ of the slab's inside part, times the slab's exact absorbance $a_j$ (S4) -- the renderers document's $\delta\zeta\,\mu\,h(x, y, \zeta_j)$ for a slab wholly inside the tube; at a column's ends, where a slab is partly inside, $a_j$ times $h$ at the middle of the inside part, so the faint limit $1.447\,a_j$ is exact for every slab
- 21.4, the evaluation: $\ell_{\rm back}$ from the quadratic of Block 2 `line_interval` (squashed section, both end cuts) with its point-direction cross term $q_0 \cdot s_0$ as a matrix product, in chunks of $2^{20}$ point-directions; then $h$ = `exp(-mu l_back) @ (W / s_z)` chunk by chunk; at $\mu = 0$, $h = \langle 1/s_z\rangle_W$ exactly (an infinite $\ell_{\rm back}$ -- an uncapped tube, a direction along its axis -- never meets $0 \cdot \infty$). `render_transmittance` computes every slab's $\Delta A^{\rm hyb}_j$ once per call and keeps each slab's box with its nonzero entries (12 bytes each), served in increasing $j$ to the direct backend's per-plane loop, the FFT path's plane chunks and the far path; the partition and `linear` keep Block 4's slabs computed on the fly (no change)
- Public API: `hybrid.back_lengths(points (n, 3) um, tube, S (N, 3), light_direction=+1) -> (n, N) um`; `hybrid.ray_history(points (n, 3) um, tube, mu, S, W, light_direction=+1) -> (n,)` ($h$, dimensionless); `hybrid.slab_absorbed(X, Y, z_lo, z_hi, zeta, dzeta, mu, tube, S, W, light_direction=+1) -> array` ($\Delta A^{\rm hyb}_j$ of one slab on the columns $(X, Y)$, broadcast to the shape of `z_lo`, with Block 2's column intervals $[z_{\rm lo}, z_{\rm hi}]$; a column that misses has $z_{\rm lo} = z_{\rm hi}$); `render.absorbed_fractions(tube, mu, grid, zeta, dzeta, light_direction=+1, absorption="partition_vertical", directions=None)` (`directions` = (S, W), required for `"hybrid"`); `render.render_transmittance(...)` with `rcfg.absorption = "hybrid"`; `config.ABSORPTIONS`. Directions must be finite unit vectors with $s_z > 0$ (to 1e-12), weights finite, >= 0, summing to 1 (ValueError otherwise)
- Parameters: `RendererConfig.absorption` (`"hybrid"` selects it; the default stays `"partition_vertical"` until the user switches it, D-084.2, D-092), `ray_na` 1.4, `ray_n_oil` 1.515, `ray_n_rho` 16, `ray_n_psi` 32 (Block 9's; `ray_n_psi` even for the light/mirror symmetry), `dzeta_um`, the kernel; `AcquisitionConfig.light_direction`
- Library calls relied on: numpy (`matmul`, `exp`, `sqrt`); Block 2 (`slab_absorbance`, `column_interval`, `Tube.t_hat0`, `Tube.e_u`); Block 9 (`directions`)
- Custom code: the matrix form of `line_interval` (no library computes chords of a capped, squashed cylinder; 56 ns per point-direction against 805 ns for `line_interval`'s broadcast form on 2 million point-directions **[run, sandbox]**), the direction average, the slab cache
- Test oracles (`tests/smoke/test_smoke_hybrid.py`): (a) known answer: vertical illumination ($W$ the point mass at $\hat s = (0, 0, 1)$): $\Delta A^{\rm hyb}_j = a_j e^{-\mu(\bar\zeta_j - z_{\rm lo})}$ (light +1) and $a_j e^{-\mu(z_{\rm hi} - \bar\zeta_j)}$ (-1) to 1e-12 on a tilted tube, that is the partition's $\Delta A_j$ times $(a_j/2)/\sinh(a_j/2)$ to 1e-12 relative; the faint limit: $h = \langle 1/s_z\rangle_W$ (Block 9 `mean_inverse_cos`, within 1e-10 of (S10)'s 1.4470) to 1e-12 at $\mu = 0$, and at $\mu$ = 1e-3/um $\Delta A^{\rm hyb}_j/(1.4470\,a_j) \in [e^{-\mu L_{\max}}, 1]$ with $L_{\max}$ the tube's longest chord, so the rendered node-plane dip is within $[-\mu L_{\max}, 0]$ (relative) of the `linear` renderer's at $1.4470\mu$; the prototype's centre dips (`checks/hybrid_absorption_check.out`, column H: 0.057, 0.450, 0.845, 0.961, 0.811, 0.732 at $\mu d$ = 0.05, 0.5, 1.5, 3, 10, 50; flat $d$ = 1 um, the node plane's centre, the cone kernel weighted $W^*$, 16 x 32 directions) from `ray_history` integrated along the cone's lines through the centre as the prototype does, within 5e-4 (its 3-decimal print); the centre column of that tube books $\sum_j \Delta A^{\rm hyb}_j$ = the column integral of $\mu h$ (Gauss-Legendre) within the midpoint rule's error, and more than 1 at $\mu d$ = 3 (a column is not conserved); (b) reference: `back_lengths` equals Block 2's `line_interval` ($\max(0, \min(t_2, 0) - t_1)$, resp. $\max(0, t_2 - \max(t_1, 0))$, 0 where the line misses) to 1e-12 at interior points and 1e-9 at all points (a near-tangent line's chord is the square root of roundoff) of tubes with both end cuts, a squashed section ($k$ = 0.7) and $\varphi$ = 0, 30, 75 deg, both light directions, and over several chunks (5000 interior points x 512 directions) to 1e-12; equals a bisection of Block 2's `inside_tube` along the ray to 1e-9 at interior points; `ray_history` equals the prototype's two-dimensional closed form (renderers document s.3.8, $\ell^{\rm 2D}_{\rm back}/p$; its `A_density`/$\mu$) to 1e-12 on a flat tube away from its caps; (c) invariants: the total booked over a flat tube's cross-section per unit length (2-D Gauss-Legendre of $\mu h$) equals the ray world's total shadow $\langle (p/s_z)\int_{-r}^{r}(1 - e^{-2\mu\sqrt{r^2 - w^2}/p})\,dw\rangle_W$ ($p = (s_v^2 + s_z^2)^{1/2}$, $s_v$ the across component; renderers document s.3.9: equal for every direction) to 1e-8 at $\mu d$ = 3 and 1e-6 at 50 (160 x 160 Gauss-Legendre nodes in $w = r\sin\alpha$ and the column; the shadow equals the prototype's printed 1.1137 and 1.2383 um to 5e-5); a rendered plane's dip volume equals the ray world's (Block 9, no post blur, 8 x 16 directions for both) on the same grid to 1e-3; light reversed = depth mirrored, $\tau(z_k; +1, \theta) = \tau(2c_z - z_k; -1, \theta + \pi)$, to 1e-12 (even `ray_n_psi`, a slab lattice that spans the depth range exactly) and the light direction matters for a dark tube; fft without the split equals direct to 1e-10 (one cache serves both); $\tau \le 1$; (d) contract: `render_transmittance` with `"hybrid"`: a `RenderResult` of the grid's shape and the reduce path; `synthetic_block` in the `fetch_zblock` contract; `validate()` accepts `"hybrid"`; the default's hashes `0508e7ff12f48728` / `0ea5a6d2ec069c68`, the hybrid's simulation hash different and its estimator hash equal; (e) determinism: two renders bit-identical; `synthetic_block` bit-identical under one seed; (f) edge cases: $\mu = 0$ gives $\tau = 1$; a tube off the grid gives $\tau = 1$ and no slab; `absorbed_fractions(..., "hybrid")` without directions, directions with $s_z \le 0$ or not unit, weights that do not sum to 1, `light_direction` 0, a negative $\mu$, points not of shape (n, 3), and `_slabs` asked for the hybrid without its inputs raise ValueError; a point on the tube's lower skin has $\ell_{\rm back} = 0$ for every direction (light +1); an uncapped tube and a direction along its axis: $\ell_{\rm back} = \infty$ inside, $h$ finite at $\mu > 0$ and $\langle 1/s_z\rangle_W$ at $\mu = 0$. Convergence: the node plane's centre dip ($d$ = 0.6 um, 10 deg, $\mu$ = 2/um) moves by less than 2e-4 when `dzeta_um` halves from 0.02 um and by less than 2e-4 when both direction counts double
- Data flow: consumes Blocks 2, 4 and 9; feeds Block 4's planes, hence Blocks 5, 6 and 14 (phantoms) and 10 and 15 (synthetic calibration) when a configuration selects it
- Confirmed: D-084 (the hybrid is the renderer to use) and D-092 ("GO on with the hybrid renderer"); (H) and (5-6) are the renderers document's Eqs. 13-14; the mode's name, 21.2, 21.3, 21.4 and the default left unchanged are the assistant's, PROVISIONAL (D-092)
- Not built: the switch of the default (D-084.2, the user's word); a cone kernel in Block 4 (the like-for-like comparisons below use it outside the pipeline, in 1-D); the fits of the renderers document's s.3.9 test 1 ($\Delta\hat d/d$, $\Delta\hat\mu$ against the ray world); a faster evaluation for the production table (section 8)
- Findings (2026-10-27; `checks/hybrid_2026-10-27/`, infinite flat tubes in 1-D across the tube, light +1, 16 x 32 directions) **[run, sandbox]**: (i) the hybrid is not positivity-preserving: with the production Gaussian kernel its planes go below zero in dark stain -- the node plane's minimum of $\tau$ is 0.036 / 0.087 / 0.117 / 0.116 at $\mu d$ = 1.5 ($d$ = 0.5 / 1 / 2 / 3 um), -0.057 / 0.005 / 0.042 / 0.040 at $\mu d$ = 2 and -0.130 / -0.096 / -0.034 / -0.013 at 3, and the planes on the light's entry side earlier (-0.062 at $d$ = 1 um, $\mu d$ = 2, $z_k = c_z - 0.28$ um); the partition's planes also go below zero there, but only from $\mu d$ = 5 off focus (-0.007 to -0.189). The cause **[reasoning; run]**: (H) conserves the light per direction over the tube, not per column -- the centre column of a flat 1 um tube books 1.03 times its own light at $\mu d$ = 1.5 and 1.18 at 3, because the oblique rays that enter through the side walls are booked where they are absorbed -- and a kernel narrower than the cone, as the production kernel is beyond about 0.1 um (renderers document (15)), does not spread that light back out. (ii) Like-for-like, with the cone's own kernel weighted $W^*$ against the exact ray world without post blur (renderers document s.3.7, s.3.9 tests 1-2 at profile level, no fit): the largest $|\tau - \tau^{\rm ray}|$ over the profile at $\mu d$ = 0.3 / 1 / 1.5 / 3 / 10 is 0.009 / 0.060 / 0.105 / 0.227 / 0.436 for the hybrid, 0.085 / 0.195 / 0.254 / 0.365 / 0.499 for the partition at the same $\mu$; the hybrid's dip area is within 0.4 % of the ray world's up to $\mu d$ = 3 (1.1 % at 10), the partition's 71-81 % (its vertical path); the hybrid goes below zero with this kernel too, at $\mu d$ = 3 on the entry side (-0.118, $d$ = 1 um, $c_z - 0.56$ um) and at $\mu d$ = 10 in the node plane (-0.19 to -0.22): the light the renderers document saw moved from the centre (s.3.9) lands near the tube's edges and overshoots there. (iii) Cost, one sandbox core, an 87 x 87 px block with 17 px padding at $h = p_{\rm x}/8$, 7 planes: $d$ 0.3 um flat 11.8 s (partition 2.8 s); 1 um flat 95.8 s (6.0 s); 1 um at 45 deg 193.5 s (9.0 s); 2 um flat 432 s (8.6 s) -- 62-94 ns per point-direction, the slab points growing as $d^2$ ($2.6\cdot10^5$, $2.9\cdot10^6$, $3.8\cdot10^6$, $1.1\cdot10^7$); at 8 x 16 directions the planes move by at most 1.2e-3 from 32 x 64 (1.8e-6 flat; 16 x 32: 3.1e-4) at a quarter of the cost. Section 8
- Status: drafted 2026-10-27; implemented 2026-10-27 (`model/hybrid.py`, the mode in `model/render.py`, `config.ABSORPTIONS`; the prototype copied from `main`; the default's hashes unchanged). `test_smoke_hybrid.py` 7 pass, 45-80 s (one core; 80 s while another job shares the machine): vertical illumination exact to 1e-12 and the partition times $(a_j/2)/\sinh(a_j/2)$; $\langle 1/s_z\rangle$ 1.447000951930 against (S10)'s 1.447000951931; the faint dip within -3.6e-4 of the `linear` renderer's at $1.447\mu$ (bound -2.6e-3); the prototype's six centre dips reproduced (0.05726, 0.45041, 0.84539, 0.96085, 0.81081, 0.73160 against its 0.057 ... 0.732); the centre column 1.18192 against the integral's 1.18226; `back_lengths` against `line_interval` 3.4e-13 inside, 2.3e-11 at all points; booking against shadow per unit length 6e-10 ($\mu d$ = 3) and 1.7e-7 (50); dip volume against the ray world -3.2e-4; light/mirror exact; the centre dip moves 3.5e-5 ($\delta\zeta$ halved) and 4.5e-7 (directions doubled); 15 of 16 mutants each failed a check, the 16th (the line's hit mask after the end cut removed) is equivalent -- the clip at 0 already gives a line that misses no length; all 36 smoke suites pass on the same tree (the partition's paths unchanged: `test_smoke_render` 7 pass) **[run, sandbox]**. Smoke-tested; the default stays the partition (D-084.2)

- The production bias table (davinci, D-022). Substitute: `n_replicates` of order 50-200 on a coarse range in the sandbox.
- The full `robustness_registration.py` takes > 2 min; it was run once here (40/40).
- The Phase II scripts against Allen's data (Block 11: `run_node.py`, `registration_survey.py`, `camera_calibration.py`, `calibrate_kernel.py real`, `run_cell.py`): substitute, a synthetic cell served through `fetch_zblock` (`tests/smoke/fixtures_cell.py`). The bootstrap's clone and pull did run against GitHub from the sandbox.
- `scripts/cell_pool.py pool` and `check` against Allen's server (Block 16.1, 2026-10-26): substitutes, canned API rows and a substituted `api_query` that answers the NeuronReconstruction, Specimen and SubImage queries (`tests/smoke/test_smoke_cell_pool.py`).
- `scripts/survey_cells.py run` against Allen's server (Block 16.2, 2026-10-26): substitute, a synthetic source serving painted projections through `SyntheticFetcher` (`tests/smoke/test_smoke_cell_alignment.py`).
- `scripts/projection_check.py` against Allen's server (Block 16.3, 2026-10-27): substitute, a synthetic source serving a synthetic stack and its exact minimum and maximum through `SyntheticFetcher`, also as JPEG (`tests/smoke/test_smoke_projection_check.py`). A WebFetch of the RMA SubImage query from the sandbox needs the user's approval (withdrawn unanswered on 2026-10-27).
- The notebook's cells that reach Allen's server (3a, 3b, 4a, 4b, 4d, 4e, 4f, 4g, 4h, 4i, 5, 6, 7, 10, 10b; 11 and 12 since 2026-10-26; 11b since 2026-10-27) and `list_stretches.py` on a real Allen SWC (2026-10-07): substitutes, the offline cells run against a stub `google.colab`, every command parsed by its script's parser, the stretch list on a hand-built SWC, and Cells 4d, 4e, 4f, 4g, 4h and 4i on synthetic cells with the fixture in place of Allen's server.

## 8. Open questions

- The calibration nodes without a straightness test (D-093, raised 2026-10-27): a node's dip takes in the tube within about $\pm 3\sigma_{\rm r}(\delta)$ along the branch: about $\pm$1.8 um at the near set's outermost planes ($\sigma_{\rm r}$(0.84 um) = 0.603 um in the default, uncalibrated table) and about $\pm$10 um at the far set's offset of 4.48 um ($\sigma_{\rm r}$ = 3.48 um with the default continuation slope 0.79; 3.22-5.01 um for slopes 0.72-1.21) **[run, sandbox: `model.kernel.sigma_r` on the default configuration, 2026-10-27]** -- so a bend within that reach enters the node's profile and its fitted width, little for the near set, up to the old test's own $\pm$10 um window for the far set **[reasoning]** [corrected 2026-10-27: first written as the near set's blur staying within about 0.3 um]. Open: whether the far set keeps a relaxed test (`node_straight_check` with 2 or 4 radii, D-093's fallback) while the near set has none; the isolation test (0.03 gl over the $\pm 25$ um square, D-081.1) is unchanged and failed most of the pilot's candidates too (the user's run of 2026-10-27).
- The calibration nodes without the faint criteria (D-094, raised 2026-10-28). (a) The optical depth: in the faint limit the dip is the kernel applied to the projected absorbance (D-026 (b)), and a dark node saturates in its centre. On noise-free flat phantoms of $d$ = 0.3 and 0.5 um rendered with the default table (the near set), on paired geometries, the near growth fit moved between $\alpha$ = 0.1 and 2 by less than 1 % in $\hat\sigma_{\rm r}$(0.84 um) (0.597-0.604 um) and by at most 1.7 % in $\hat\sigma_{\rm r}$(0.56 um), five phantoms per ($d$, $\alpha$), under the partition; under Block 21's hybrid ($d$ = 0.3 um, $\alpha$ = 0.1, 1, 1.5, four phantoms) by 0.7 % and 1.0 %. At the one-plane knot, whose growth is 0.0085 um$^2$ in the table and already within Phase I's bias, $\hat G$(0.28 um) moved by up to 0.005 um$^2$ at $d$ = 0.5 um ($\hat\sigma_{\rm r}$ 0.106 -> 0.081 um) **[run, sandbox: `checks/d094_2026-10-28/dark_node_growth.py`, its `.out` and `_hybrid.out`]**. Not tested: whether either renderer images a dark tube as the microscope does (T7; the partition's profiles differ from the ray world's by up to 0.20-0.25 in $\tau$ at $\mu d$ = 1-1.5, D-092.3 (c)); the far set, where D-026 (e) put the cut for dark thick tubes; noise. (b) The flag `faint`: such a node's in-focus dip is under 6 gl on the pilot's plane, so its defocused planes fall to a few noise SDs; whether their core fits add only scatter or also a low-SNR bias to the growth is not measured **[reasoning]**; plane statuses and `min_planes_per_node` drop the planes and nodes whose fits fail. A by-product of the same run, independent of $\alpha$: at $\alpha$ = 0.1 the fitted growth sits below Phase I's oracle (the projected faint-limit growth of a tube of that $d$) by 0.020 um$^2$ ($d$ = 0.3 um) and 0.047 um$^2$ ($d$ = 0.5 um) at 0.56 um, four ($d$ = 0.3 um) to eleven ($d$ = 0.5 um) times the largest $\alpha$ effect there, so the first-stage table at two planes comes out 0.389 and 0.357 um against the configured 0.438 um (Phase I, $d$ = 0.25 um: 0.399): within the near set ($D_{\rm A} \le$ 0.6 um), a node's thickness moves the table far more than its darkness **[run, sandbox: the same `.out`, row `proj`]**; the first stage is known to need step 2 of procedure s.3.4 (the item "Kernel calibration, step 2" below, not built), and this sizes the need for the near set's thicker nodes. Open: whether the far set keeps $\hat\alpha \le 0.5$ (`node_alpha_check`) while the near set has none.
- The hybrid (Block 21, raised 2026-10-27, for the user and T7): (a) it is not positivity-preserving -- with the production kernel its planes go below zero from $\mu d \approx 2$ (the node plane) and earlier on the light's entry side, while the phantoms' design reaches $\mu d$ = 12 ($\mu \le$ 3/um, $d \le$ 4 um) (Block 21's findings (i)-(ii)); like-for-like it is much closer to the ray world than the partition up to $\mu d \approx 1.5$ and goes below zero beyond. Options, none decided: keep the partition (D-024 (i)); use the hybrid and limit the phantoms' $\mu d$ to where it stays positive, once S3a's real $\hat\mu_i\hat d_i$ distribution (Cell 13) shows how many real nodes lie beyond; a variant that conserves light per column or per line (to be derived, T7); clip $\tau$ at 0 (the camera chain clips grey levels at 0 already, which hides the overshoot without removing it). (b) The switch of the default (D-084.2): now (the simulation hash changes: 5e5b04a2f154e1c8), at S3d's final configuration, or not until (a) is settled. (c) The production cost: 4 to 50 times the partition's per phantom, growing with $d^2$, minutes per thick phantom on one core (Block 21's findings (iii)); a field of the hybrid's own for its quadrature (8 x 16: a quarter of the cost, $\tau$ within 1.2e-3) would change the simulation hash of the hybrid's configuration only; further gains need the tube's translation invariance or a coarser depth sampling of $h$ (not built). (d) The kernel under the hybrid: in the faint limit the hybrid books $1.447\,a_j$ uniformly, the partition's faint booking at $1.447\mu$, so a kernel calibrated through the partition's model on faint nodes carries over with $\hat\mu$ rescaled **[reasoning]**; in dark stain open (D-084's kernel question, T7).
