- The SGT fit's quadrature (raised 2026-10-18, Block 12): D-052.2's $N$ = 848 was derived for the Gaussian, and Gauss-Legendre converges only algebraically on the non-analytic members of the SGT family (the skew, non-even $\beta$, the Laplace) and pre-asymptotically on the box-like $\beta$ = 10; at the box corner ($d$ 6 um, $w$ 0.1 um) the model errs by up to 2.1 grey levels (and reaches -7.5 gl), in the fitted regime ($w \ge$ 0.3 um, $d \le$ 5.5 um) by at most 0.07 gl, with a bias of the IRLS $\hat d$ of at most 7.5e-4 um. Kept as decided. Alternatives, each a change of the estimator: a larger $N$ (cost linear in $N$); splitting the $\psi$ integral of (B3.1) at the kernel's peak, $\psi^* = \arcsin(2(v - v_0)/d)$ for $|v - v_0| < d/2$, which restores fast convergence for the two-piece kink (a change to `tube_model.model_profile_kernel`, shared with Block 11's `fit_variants`); an $N$ set by $w_{\rm lo}$ and the shape class. For the user (standby list)

- The phantoms' radius under D-058 (raised 2026-10-18, later): open are $\sigma_r$ (um); whether $r_n$ feeds only the iteration's start (round 1's line, half-length $3 r_n$, handoff (H6)) or also the pilot's focus window and the two masks (D-053 (i) had used one radius in all four places); the rule for $r_n \le 0$ ($d_n/2$ = 0.1 um at $d_{\min}$): a redraw or a floor; $\epsilon$ per draw or per realization (D-059); whether D-053 (i)'s sensitivity check, with its in-focus images, still runs as a diagnostic. The assistant's reservation is in D-058's Why: real nodes start from Allen's radius, whose error is systematic (Allen's $2r$ about 0.5 um in every bin of $\hat d$, the iteration bullet below), not zero-mean.
- The design of D-059 and D-060 (raised 2026-10-18, later): open are $G$ and $k$ (Q6); cross-validation folds by draw (proposed: a fold that holds some of a draw's realizations scores the spline on near-copies of its training rows); the failure rate's third coordinate for realizations without a usable $\hat\alpha$ (`profile_nan` and `bbar_few` leave no fit [corrected 2026-10-19: a `bbar_few` node is fitted -- `measure_node` fits whenever the profile is finite and $\bar B > 0$ and sets `bbar_few` apart -- so only `profile_nan`, $\bar B \le 0$ and a failed fit leave no $\hat\alpha$]; a collapse's $\hat\alpha$ is set by the $\mu$ bound) -- proposed: the true $\alpha_g = \mu_g d_g/\cos\varphi_g$ for every realization, evaluated at a real node's $\hat\alpha_i$ (the same plug-in as the line-fit $\varphi_i$); the form of D-059's coverage check (proposed: fitted against fitted per bin of $\hat d$, equivalently $\hat\mu$). Evidence behind D-060: within one truth, the errors of $\hat d$ and $\hat\alpha$ are anticorrelated on thin tubes, corr -0.94 and -0.90 at 0.6 um with $\hat\alpha$'s relative SD 37 % and 43 % (-0.72 to -0.88, 2-3 % at 1.5 um) (`checks/estimator_2026-10-15/ident_sgt/identifiability_sgt.json`, `mc[*].raw.sgt_l050`, $\mathcal S$ by D-053 (ii)). At an equal number of measured replicates, $k$ = 5 realizations per draw gave a table SD 1.06 times (no draw-level effect) and 1.34 times (a draw-level offset with $\rho$ = 0.2, as the design effect $1 + (k-1)\rho$ predicts) that of independent draws (a check run in the table chat, fixed smoothing, 60 tables per arm).
- Findings on Steps 4-5 (raised 2026-10-18, later; checks run in the table chat on the D-053/D-054 design: the fixture $b = 1 + 0.06/d + 0.15\sin^2\varphi + 0.05\ln\alpha$, $\hat\alpha$ set to the true $\alpha$, a heteroscedastic $\tau(d)$ from 0.30 at 0.25 um to 0.009 above 1.5 um, 20 tables of $N_{\mathcal S}$ = 1500; none decided):
  (a) The SE of section 2.4, $\hat\tau/\sqrt{N_{\rm eff}}$, is the standard error of a local kernel average. The spline pools far wider (92 % of its absolute weight from replicates beyond two bandwidths; its own effective count $1/\sum_n \ell_n^2$ is 89-323 against the kernel's 12-26), so at a fixed smoothing the SE is 1.2-2.8 times the spline's own SD. But the cross-validated smoothing is set by the noisy thin end (10 in 14 of 20 seeds, against 1 in 18 of 20 with a constant $\tau$), which leaves a smoothing bias at the thick end larger than the SE (-0.0033 against 0.0023 at $d$ = 3 um, $\varphi$ = 45 deg, $\hat\alpha$ = 4). Options: keep the proxy; report the spline's own $\sqrt{\sum_n \ell_n^2 \hat\tau^2(x_n)}$; bootstrap the rows with the CV re-run.
  (b) Off-support roots: at fixed $(\varphi_i, \hat\alpha_i)$ the phantoms cover only $d \in [\hat\alpha_i\cos\varphi_i/\mu_{\max},\ \hat\alpha_i\cos\varphi_i/\mu_{\min}]$, at most 77 % of the bracket in $\ln d$; a node darker than every phantom of its size is inverted with no flag (a 0.5 um node at 4 /um: root 0.492 um; there $N_{\rm eff}$ = 9.6, inside the in-band range, but the kernel mass $\sum_n w_n$ = 2.05, below the replicates' own leave-one-out 5th percentile, 3.06). Proposal: an `off_support` flag on the kernel mass at the root.
  (c) The $\ln\hat\alpha$ range of D-054 (i)'s normalisation: the retained rows' min-max is set by near-vertical draws (the 1st-99th percentile span is 69 % of it). Proposal: a percentile or a nominal range.
  (d) One smoothing for a heteroscedastic table: a per-point smoothing proportional to $\hat\tau^2(x_n)$ (`RBFInterpolator` accepts an array) would weight the thin end down. Proposal, untested.
  (e) `high_failure`: at $N_{\rm eff}$ 15-28, $\hat p_{\rm fail}$'s binomial noise makes the strict flag at 0.2 fire for 45-47 % of nodes whose failure probability is 0.2 and for 5-11 % at 0.1 (for D-054 (iii)'s pilot).
